I've uploaded a python code repository in the directory juanifioren__django-oidc-provider__0.7. Consider the following issue description:

<issue_description>
Handle optional scope on token and introspection endpoints for password and client_credentials grant_type
Parameter scope is defined as optional on authorization https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc6749#section-4.1.1 and introspection https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc7662 requests. 

An enhancement is proposed to handle `scope` parameter on authorization requests with grant_type=password or grant_type= client_credentials (related to https://github.com/juanifioren/django-oidc-provider/pull/193):
1) if `scope` is not provided client's scope(s) are assigned to access token - 

- grant_type=client_credentials: current behavior of django-oidc-provider
- grant_type=password: currently django-oidc-provider assigns empty scope

2) if valid (supported by given client) scopes are requested requested scopes are assigned to access token
3) if requested scope(s) are not supported `raise TokenError('invalid_scope')`

An enhancement is proposed to display optional `scope` in introspection response controlled by new settings variable `OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE`. 
</issue_description>

Can you help me implement the necessary changes to the repository so that the requirements specified in the <issue_description> are met?

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me carefully read and understand the issue description:

1. The RFC 6749 defines the scope parameter as optional on authorization requests
2. The RFC 7662 defines scope as optional on introspection requests
3. Currently django-oidc-provider has inconsistent behavior:
   - For grant_type=client_credentials: assigns client's scope(s) to access token when scope not provided
   - For grant_type=password: assigns empty scope when scope not provided (this needs to be fixed)

The requirements are:
1. When scope is not provided:
   - For client_credentials: keep current behavior (assign client's scopes)
   - For password: assign client's scopes (instead of empty scope)
2. When valid scopes are requested: assign those scopes to access token
3. When invalid scopes are requested: raise TokenError('invalid_scope')
4. Add support for displaying optional scope in introspection response controlled by OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE setting

Key technical details:
- Methods involved: token endpoint handling, introspection endpoint handling
- Error type: TokenError with message 'invalid_scope'
- Settings variable: OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE
- Grant types affected: password and client_credentials

Best practices to consider:
- Minimal changes to existing functionality
- Backward compatibility
- Proper error handling
- Following OAuth2/OIDC standards</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 1.1-1.3 Problem Analysis

The issue describes enhancements to handle optional `scope` parameter on OAuth2 token and introspection endpoints for `password` and `client_credentials` grant types. 

**Current Issues:**
- For `grant_type=password`: when scope is not provided, empty scope is assigned (should assign client's scopes)
- For `grant_type=client_credentials`: already works correctly (assigns client's scopes when scope not provided)
- Missing proper validation of requested scopes against client's allowed scopes
- Missing scope information in introspection responses

**Requirements:**
1. Handle optional scope parameter on token requests for password/client_credentials grants
2. When scope not provided: assign client's scopes to access token  
3. When valid scopes provided: assign requested scopes
4. When invalid scopes provided: raise `TokenError('invalid_scope')`
5. Display optional scope in introspection response controlled by `OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE` setting

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Make token requests with `grant_type=password` without scope parameter (should get empty scope currently)
2. Make token requests with `grant_type=client_credentials` without scope parameter (should work correctly)
3. Make token requests with invalid scopes for either grant type (should validate properly)
4. Make introspection requests and check if scope is returned in response

### 1.5 Best Practices
- Maintain backward compatibility
- Follow OAuth2/OIDC RFC specifications
- Proper error handling with appropriate error messages
- Minimal code changes
- Comprehensive testing of edge cases

     9	
    10	OpenID Connect is a simple identity layer on top of the OAuth 2.0 protocol, which allows computing clients to verify the identity of an end-user based on the authentication performed by an authorization server, as well as to obtain basic profile information about the end-user in an interoperable and REST-like manner.      1	import inspect
     2	from base64 import urlsafe_b64encode
     3	import hashlib
     4	import logging
     5	from django.contrib.auth import authenticate
     6	
     7	from django.http import JsonResponse
     8	
     9	from oidc_provider.lib.errors import (
    10	    TokenError,
    11	    UserAuthError,
    12	)
    13	from oidc_provider.lib.utils.oauth2 import extract_client_auth
    14	from oidc_provider.lib.utils.token import (
    15	    create_id_token,
    16	    create_token,
    17	    encode_id_token,
    18	)
    19	from oidc_provider.models import (
    20	    Client,
    21	    Code,
    22	    Token,
    23	)
    24	from oidc_provider import settings
    25	
    26	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    27	
    28	
    29	class TokenEndpoint(object):
    30	
    31	    def __init__(self, request):
    32	        self.request = request
    33	        self.params = {}
    34	        self.user = None
    35	        self._extract_params()
    36	
    37	    def _extract_params(self):
    38	        client_id, client_secret = extract_client_auth(self.request)
    39	
    40	        self.params['client_id'] = client_id
    41	        self.params['client_secret'] = client_secret
    42	        self.params['redirect_uri'] = self.request.POST.get('redirect_uri', '')
    43	        self.params['grant_type'] = self.request.POST.get('grant_type', '')
    44	        self.params['code'] = self.request.POST.get('code', '')
    45	        self.params['state'] = self.request.POST.get('state', '')
    46	        self.params['scope'] = self.request.POST.get('scope', '')
    47	        self.params['refresh_token'] = self.request.POST.get('refresh_token', '')
    48	        # PKCE parameter.
    49	        self.params['code_verifier'] = self.request.POST.get('code_verifier')
    50	
    51	        self.params['username'] = self.request.POST.get('username', '')
    52	        self.params['password'] = self.request.POST.get('password', '')
    53	
    54	    def validate_params(self):
    55	        try:
    56	            self.client = Client.objects.get(client_id=self.params['client_id'])
    57	        except Client.DoesNotExist:
    58	            logger.debug('[Token] Client does not exist: %s', self.params['client_id'])
    59	            raise TokenError('invalid_client')
    60	
    61	        if self.client.client_type == 'confidential':
    62	            if not (self.client.client_secret == self.params['client_secret']):
    63	                logger.debug('[Token] Invalid client secret: client %s do not have secret %s',
    64	                             self.client.client_id, self.client.client_secret)
    65	                raise TokenError('invalid_client')
    66	
    67	        if self.params['grant_type'] == 'authorization_code':
    68	            if not (self.params['redirect_uri'] in self.client.redirect_uris):
    69	                logger.debug('[Token] Invalid redirect uri: %s', self.params['redirect_uri'])
    70	                raise TokenError('invalid_client')
    71	
    72	            try:
    73	                self.code = Code.objects.get(code=self.params['code'])
    74	            except Code.DoesNotExist:
    75	                logger.debug('[Token] Code does not exist: %s', self.params['code'])
    76	                raise TokenError('invalid_grant')
    77	
    78	            if not (self.code.client == self.client) \
    79	               or self.code.has_expired():
    80	                logger.debug('[Token] Invalid code: invalid client or code has expired')
    81	                raise TokenError('invalid_grant')
    82	
    83	            # Validate PKCE parameters.
    84	            if self.params['code_verifier']:
    85	                if self.code.code_challenge_method == 'S256':
    86	                    new_code_challenge = urlsafe_b64encode(
    87	                            hashlib.sha256(self.params['code_verifier'].encode('ascii')).digest()
    88	                        ).decode('utf-8').replace('=', '')
    89	                else:
    90	                    new_code_challenge = self.params['code_verifier']
    91	
    92	                # TODO: We should explain the error.
    93	                if not (new_code_challenge == self.code.code_challenge):
    94	                    raise TokenError('invalid_grant')
    95	
    96	        elif self.params['grant_type'] == 'password':
    97	            if not settings.get('OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE'):
    98	                raise TokenError('unsupported_grant_type')
    99	
   100	            auth_args = (self.request,)
   101	            try:
   102	                inspect.getcallargs(authenticate, *auth_args)
   103	            except TypeError:
   104	                auth_args = ()
   105	
   106	            user = authenticate(
   107	                *auth_args,
   108	                username=self.params['username'],
   109	                password=self.params['password']
   110	            )
   111	
   112	            if not user:
   113	                raise UserAuthError()
   114	
   115	            self.user = user
   116	
   117	        elif self.params['grant_type'] == 'refresh_token':
   118	            if not self.params['refresh_token']:
   119	                logger.debug('[Token] Missing refresh token')
   120	                raise TokenError('invalid_grant')
   121	
   122	            try:
   123	                self.token = Token.objects.get(refresh_token=self.params['refresh_token'],
   124	                                               client=self.client)
   125	
   126	            except Token.DoesNotExist:
   127	                logger.debug(
   128	                    '[Token] Refresh token does not exist: %s', self.params['refresh_token'])
   129	                raise TokenError('invalid_grant')
   130	        elif self.params['grant_type'] == 'client_credentials':
   131	            if not self.client._scope:
   132	                logger.debug('[Token] Client using client credentials with empty scope')
   133	                raise TokenError('invalid_scope')
   134	        else:
   135	            logger.debug('[Token] Invalid grant type: %s', self.params['grant_type'])
   136	            raise TokenError('unsupported_grant_type')
   137	
   138	    def create_response_dic(self):
   139	        if self.params['grant_type'] == 'authorization_code':
   140	            return self.create_code_response_dic()
   141	        elif self.params['grant_type'] == 'refresh_token':
   142	            return self.create_refresh_response_dic()
   143	        elif self.params['grant_type'] == 'password':
   144	            return self.create_access_token_response_dic()
   145	        elif self.params['grant_type'] == 'client_credentials':
   146	            return self.create_client_credentials_response_dic()
   147	
   148	    def create_code_response_dic(self):
   149	        # See https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc6749#section-4.1
   150	
   151	        token = create_token(
   152	            user=self.code.user,
   153	            client=self.code.client,
   154	            scope=self.code.scope)
   155	
   156	        if self.code.is_authentication:
   157	            id_token_dic = create_id_token(
   158	                user=self.code.user,
   159	                aud=self.client.client_id,
   160	                token=token,
   161	                nonce=self.code.nonce,
   162	                at_hash=token.at_hash,
   163	                request=self.request,
   164	                scope=token.scope,
   165	            )
   166	        else:
   167	            id_token_dic = {}
   168	        token.id_token = id_token_dic
   169	
   170	        # Store the token.
   171	        token.save()
   172	
   173	        # We don't need to store the code anymore.
   174	        self.code.delete()
   175	
   176	        dic = {
   177	            'access_token': token.access_token,
   178	            'refresh_token': token.refresh_token,
   179	            'token_type': 'bearer',
   180	            'expires_in': settings.get('OIDC_TOKEN_EXPIRE'),
   181	            'id_token': encode_id_token(id_token_dic, token.client),
   182	        }
   183	
   184	        return dic
   185	
   186	    def create_refresh_response_dic(self):
   187	        # See https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc6749#section-6
   188	
   189	        scope_param = self.params['scope']
   190	        scope = (scope_param.split(' ') if scope_param else self.token.scope)
   191	        unauthorized_scopes = set(scope) - set(self.token.scope)
   192	        if unauthorized_scopes:
   193	            raise TokenError('invalid_scope')
   194	
   195	        token = create_token(
   196	            user=self.token.user,
   197	            client=self.token.client,
   198	            scope=scope)
   199	
   200	        # If the Token has an id_token it's an Authentication request.
   201	        if self.token.id_token:
   202	            id_token_dic = create_id_token(
   203	                user=self.token.user,
   204	                aud=self.client.client_id,
   205	                token=token,
   206	                nonce=None,
   207	                at_hash=token.at_hash,
   208	                request=self.request,
   209	                scope=token.scope,
   210	            )
   211	        else:
   212	            id_token_dic = {}
   213	        token.id_token = id_token_dic
   214	
   215	        # Store the token.
   216	        token.save()
   217	
   218	        # Forget the old token.
   219	        self.token.delete()
   220	
   221	        dic = {
   222	            'access_token': token.access_token,
   223	            'refresh_token': token.refresh_token,
   224	            'token_type': 'bearer',
   225	            'expires_in': settings.get('OIDC_TOKEN_EXPIRE'),
   226	            'id_token': encode_id_token(id_token_dic, self.token.client),
   227	        }
   228	
   229	        return dic
   230	
   231	    def create_access_token_response_dic(self):
   232	        # See https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc6749#section-4.3
   233	
   234	        token = create_token(
   235	            self.user,
   236	            self.client,
   237	            self.params['scope'].split(' '))
   238	
   239	        id_token_dic = create_id_token(
   240	            token=token,
   241	            user=self.user,
   242	            aud=self.client.client_id,
   243	            nonce='self.code.nonce',
   244	            at_hash=token.at_hash,
   245	            request=self.request,
   246	            scope=token.scope,
   247	        )
   248	
   249	        token.id_token = id_token_dic
   250	        token.save()
   251	
   252	        return {
   253	            'access_token': token.access_token,
   254	            'refresh_token': token.refresh_token,
   255	            'expires_in': settings.get('OIDC_TOKEN_EXPIRE'),
   256	            'token_type': 'bearer',
   257	            'id_token': encode_id_token(id_token_dic, token.client),
   258	        }
   259	
   260	    def create_client_credentials_response_dic(self):
   261	        # See https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc6749#section-4.4.3
   262	
   263	        token = create_token(
   264	            user=None,
   265	            client=self.client,
   266	            scope=self.client.scope)
   267	
   268	        token.save()
   269	
   270	        return {
   271	            'access_token': token.access_token,
   272	            'expires_in': settings.get('OIDC_TOKEN_EXPIRE'),
   273	            'token_type': 'bearer',
   274	            'scope': self.client._scope,
   275	        }
   276	
   277	    @classmethod
   278	    def response(cls, dic, status=200):
   279	        """
   280	        Create and return a response object.
   281	        """
   282	        response = JsonResponse(dic, status=status)
   283	        response['Cache-Control'] = 'no-store'
   284	        response['Pragma'] = 'no-cache'
   285	
   286	        return response
   287

     1	import logging
     2	
     3	from django.http import JsonResponse
     4	
     5	from oidc_provider.lib.errors import TokenIntrospectionError
     6	from oidc_provider.lib.utils.common import run_processing_hook
     7	from oidc_provider.lib.utils.oauth2 import extract_client_auth
     8	from oidc_provider.models import Token, Client
     9	from oidc_provider import settings
    10	
    11	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    12	
    13	INTROSPECTION_SCOPE = 'token_introspection'
    14	
    15	
    16	class TokenIntrospectionEndpoint(object):
    17	
    18	    def __init__(self, request):
    19	        self.request = request
    20	        self.params = {}
    21	        self.token = None
    22	        self.id_token = None
    23	        self.client = None
    24	        self._extract_params()
    25	
    26	    def _extract_params(self):
    27	        # Introspection only supports POST requests
    28	        self.params['token'] = self.request.POST.get('token')
    29	        client_id, client_secret = extract_client_auth(self.request)
    30	        self.params['client_id'] = client_id
    31	        self.params['client_secret'] = client_secret
    32	
    33	    def validate_params(self):
    34	        if not (self.params['client_id'] and self.params['client_secret']):
    35	            logger.debug('[Introspection] No client credentials provided')
    36	            raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    37	        if not self.params['token']:
    38	            logger.debug('[Introspection] No token provided')
    39	            raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    40	        try:
    41	            self.token = Token.objects.get(access_token=self.params['token'])
    42	        except Token.DoesNotExist:
    43	            logger.debug('[Introspection] Token does not exist: %s', self.params['token'])
    44	            raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    45	        if self.token.has_expired():
    46	            logger.debug('[Introspection] Token is not valid: %s', self.params['token'])
    47	            raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    48	
    49	        try:
    50	            self.client = Client.objects.get(
    51	                client_id=self.params['client_id'],
    52	                client_secret=self.params['client_secret'])
    53	        except Client.DoesNotExist:
    54	            logger.debug('[Introspection] No valid client for id: %s',
    55	                         self.params['client_id'])
    56	            raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    57	        if INTROSPECTION_SCOPE not in self.client.scope:
    58	            logger.debug('[Introspection] Client %s does not have introspection scope',
    59	                         self.params['client_id'])
    60	            raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    61	
    62	        self.id_token = self.token.id_token
    63	
    64	        if settings.get('OIDC_INTROSPECTION_VALIDATE_AUDIENCE_SCOPE'):
    65	            if not self.token.id_token:
    66	                logger.debug('[Introspection] Token not an authentication token: %s',
    67	                             self.params['token'])
    68	                raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    69	
    70	            audience = self.token.id_token.get('aud')
    71	            if not audience:
    72	                logger.debug('[Introspection] No audience found for token: %s',
    73	                             self.params['token'])
    74	                raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    75	
    76	            if audience not in self.client.scope:
    77	                logger.debug('[Introspection] Client %s does not audience scope %s',
    78	                             self.params['client_id'], audience)
    79	                raise TokenIntrospectionError()
    80	
    81	    def create_response_dic(self):
    82	        response_dic = {}
    83	        if self.id_token:
    84	            for k in ('aud', 'sub', 'exp', 'iat', 'iss'):
    85	                response_dic[k] = self.id_token[k]
    86	        response_dic['active'] = True
    87	        response_dic['client_id'] = self.token.client.client_id
    88	
    89	        response_dic = run_processing_hook(response_dic,
    90	                                           'OIDC_INTROSPECTION_PROCESSING_HOOK',
    91	                                           client=self.client,
    92	                                           id_token=self.id_token)
    93	
    94	        return response_dic
    95	
    96	    @classmethod
    97	    def response(cls, dic, status=200):
    98	        """
    99	        Create and return a response object.
   100	        """
   101	        response = JsonResponse(dic, status=status)
   102	        response['Cache-Control'] = 'no-store'
   103	        response['Pragma'] = 'no-cache'
   104	
   105	        return response
   106

   175	def import_from_str(value):
   176	    """
   177	    Attempt to import a class from a string representation.
   178	    """
   179	    try:
   180	        parts = value.split('.')
   181	        module_path, class_name = '.'.join(parts[:-1]), parts[-1]
   182	        module = importlib.import_module(module_path)
   183	        return getattr(module, class_name)
   184	    except ImportError as e:
   185	        msg = 'Could not import %s for settings. %s: %s.' % (value, e.__class__.__name__, e)
   186	        raise ImportError(msg)
   187	
   188	
   189	def get(name, import_str=False):
   190	    """
   191	    Helper function to use inside the package.
   192	    """
   193	    value = None
   194	    default_value = getattr(default_settings, name)
   195	
   196	    try:
   197	        value = getattr(settings, name)
   198	    except AttributeError:
   199	        if name in default_settings.required_attrs:
   200	            raise Exception('You must set ' + name + ' in your settings.')
   201	
   202	    if isinstance(default_value, dict) and value:
   203	        default_value.update(value)
   204	        value = default_value
   205	    else:
   206	        if value is None:
   207	            value = default_value
   208	        value = import_from_str(value) if import_str else value
   209	
   210	    return value

     2	import time
     3	import uuid
     4	
     5	from Cryptodome.PublicKey.RSA import importKey
     6	from django.utils import dateformat, timezone
     7	from jwkest.jwk import RSAKey as jwk_RSAKey
     8	from jwkest.jwk import SYMKey
     9	from jwkest.jws import JWS
    10	from jwkest.jwt import JWT
    11	
    12	from oidc_provider.lib.utils.common import get_issuer, run_processing_hook
    13	from oidc_provider.lib.claims import StandardScopeClaims
    14	from oidc_provider.models import (
    15	    Code,
    16	    RSAKey,
    17	    Token,
    18	)
    19	from oidc_provider import settings

    21	
    22	def create_id_token(token, user, aud, nonce='', at_hash='', request=None, scope=None):
    23	    """
    24	    Creates the id_token dictionary.
    25	    See: http://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html#IDToken
    26	    Return a dic.
    27	    """
    28	    if scope is None:
    29	        scope = []
    30	    sub = settings.get('OIDC_IDTOKEN_SUB_GENERATOR', import_str=True)(user=user)
    31	
    32	    expires_in = settings.get('OIDC_IDTOKEN_EXPIRE')
    33	
    34	    # Convert datetimes into timestamps.
    35	    now = int(time.time())
    36	    iat_time = now
    37	    exp_time = int(now + expires_in)
    38	    user_auth_time = user.last_login or user.date_joined
    39	    auth_time = int(dateformat.format(user_auth_time, 'U'))
    40	
    41	    dic = {
    42	        'iss': get_issuer(request=request),
    43	        'sub': sub,
    44	        'aud': str(aud),
    45	        'exp': exp_time,
    46	        'iat': iat_time,
    47	        'auth_time': auth_time,
    48	    }
    49	
    50	    if nonce:
    51	        dic['nonce'] = str(nonce)
    52	
    53	    if at_hash:
    54	        dic['at_hash'] = at_hash
    55	
    56	    # Inlude (or not) user standard claims in the id_token.
    57	    if settings.get('OIDC_IDTOKEN_INCLUDE_CLAIMS'):
    58	        standard_claims = StandardScopeClaims(token)
    59	        dic.update(standard_claims.create_response_dic())
    60	        if settings.get('OIDC_EXTRA_SCOPE_CLAIMS'):
    61	            custom_claims = settings.get('OIDC_EXTRA_SCOPE_CLAIMS', import_str=True)(token)
    62	            dic.update(custom_claims.create_response_dic())
    63	
    64	    dic = run_processing_hook(
    65	        dic, 'OIDC_IDTOKEN_PROCESSING_HOOK',
    66	        user=user, token=token, request=request)
    67	
    68	    return dic
    69	
    70	
    71	def encode_id_token(payload, client):
    72	    """
    73	    Represent the ID Token as a JSON Web Token (JWT).
    74	    Return a hash.
    75	    """
    76	    keys = get_client_alg_keys(client)
    77	    _jws = JWS(payload, alg=client.jwt_alg)
    78	    return _jws.sign_compact(keys)
    79	
    80	
    81	def decode_id_token(token, client):
    82	    """
    83	    Represent the ID Token as a JSON Web Token (JWT).
    84	    Return a hash.
    85	    """
    86	    keys = get_client_alg_keys(client)
    87	    return JWS().verify_compact(token, keys=keys)
    88	
    89	
    90	def client_id_from_id_token(id_token):
    91	    """
    92	    Extracts the client id from a JSON Web Token (JWT).
    93	    Returns a string or None.
    94	    """
    95	    payload = JWT().unpack(id_token).payload()
    96	    aud = payload.get('aud', None)
    97	    if aud is None:
    98	        return None
    99	    if isinstance(aud, list):
   100	        return aud[0]
   101	    return aud
   102	
   103	
   104	def create_token(user, client, scope, id_token_dic=None):
   105	    """
   106	    Create and populate a Token object.
   107	    Return a Token object.
   108	    """
   109	    token = Token()
   110	    token.user = user
   111	    token.client = client
   112	    token.access_token = uuid.uuid4().hex
   113	
   114	    if id_token_dic is not None:
   115	        token.id_token = id_token_dic
   116	
   117	    token.refresh_token = uuid.uuid4().hex
   118	    token.expires_at = timezone.now() + timedelta(
   119	        seconds=settings.get('OIDC_TOKEN_EXPIRE'))
   120	    token.scope = scope
   121	
   122	    return token
   123	
   124	
   125	def create_code(user, client, scope, nonce, is_authentication,
   126	                code_challenge=None, code_challenge_method=None):
   127	    """
   128	    Create and populate a Code object.
   129	    Return a Code object.
   130	    """
   131	    code = Code()
   132	    code.user = user
   133	    code.client = client
   134	
   135	    code.code = uuid.uuid4().hex
   136	
   137	    if code_challenge and code_challenge_method:
   138	        code.code_challenge = code_challenge
   139	        code.code_challenge_method = code_challenge_method
   140	
   141	    code.expires_at = timezone.now() + timedelta(
   142	        seconds=settings.get('OIDC_CODE_EXPIRE'))
   143	    code.scope = scope
   144	    code.nonce = nonce
   145	    code.is_authentication = is_authentication
   146	
   147	    return code
   148	
   149	
   150	def get_client_alg_keys(client):
   151	    """
   152	    Takes a client and returns the set of keys associated with it.
   153	    Returns a list of keys.
   154	    """
   155	    if client.jwt_alg == 'RS256':
   156	        keys = []
   157	        for rsakey in RSAKey.objects.all():
   158	            keys.append(jwk_RSAKey(key=importKey(rsakey.key), kid=rsakey.kid))
   159	        if not keys:
   160	            raise Exception('You must add at least one RSA Key.')
   161	    elif client.jwt_alg == 'HS256':
   162	        keys = [SYMKey(key=client.client_secret, alg=client.jwt_alg)]
   163	    else:
   164	        raise Exception('Unsupported key algorithm.')
   165	
   166	    return keys
   167

    def create_response_dic(self):

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now let me understand the issue better. Let me check the current behavior of the password grant when scope is not provided:<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Based on my analysis of the code, here's what I understand:

CURRENT BEHAVIOR:
1. For `grant_type=password` (line 231-258 in token.py): 
   - Always uses `self.params['scope'].split(' ')` even when scope is empty (line 237)
   - This results in [''] when no scope is provided, which is essentially an empty scope

2. For `grant_type=client_credentials` (line 260-275 in token.py):
   - Uses `self.client.scope` when no scope is provided (line 266)
   - This is the correct behavior according to the issue description

3. For introspection (introspection.py):
   - Does not include scope in the response currently
   - Need to add optional scope display controlled by new setting `OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE`

ISSUES TO FIX:
1. Fix password grant to use client's scope when no scope is provided (instead of empty scope)
2. Add scope validation for both password and client_credentials grants
3. Add optional scope display in introspection responses

FILES TO MODIFY:
1. `/workspace/juanifioren__django-oidc-provider__0.7/oidc_provider/lib/endpoints/token.py` - Main logic for token handling
2. `/workspace/juanifioren__django-oidc-provider__0.7/oidc_provider/lib/endpoints/introspection.py` - Add scope to response
3. `/workspace/juanifioren__django-oidc-provider__0.7/oidc_provider/settings.py` - Add new setting for introspection scope

Let me first create a simple reproduction script to understand the current behavior:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/juanifioren__django-oidc-provider__0.7/reproduce_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python
"""
Script to reproduce the issue with scope handling in token and introspection endpoints.
"""

import os
import sys
import django
from django.conf import settings
from django.test.utils import get_runner

# Add the project root to Python path
sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace/juanifioren__django-oidc-provider__0.7')

# Configure Django settings
os.environ.setdefault('DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE', 'oidc_provider.tests.settings')
django.setup()

from django.test import TestCase, Client, override_settings
from django.core.management import call_command
import json

from oidc_provider.tests.app.utils import create_fake_user, create_fake_client
from oidc_provider.models import Client


def test_password_grant_without_scope():
    """Test password grant without scope parameter."""
    print("=== Testing password grant WITHOUT scope ===")
    
    # Setup
    call_command('creatersakey')
    user = create_fake_user()
    client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
    
    # Enable password grant
    with override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True):
        c = Client()
        
        # Test without scope parameter
        post_data = {
            'username': 'johndoe',
            'password': '1234',
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'client_id': client.client_id,
            'client_secret': client.client_secret,
        }
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', data=post_data)
        print(f"Response status: {response.status_code}")
        print(f"Response content: {response.content.decode('utf-8')}")
        
        if response.status_code == 200:
            response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
            access_token = response_dict.get('access_token')
            if access_token:
                # Check what scopes were actually assigned to the token
                from oidc_provider.models import Token
                try:
                    token = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
                    print(f"Token scopes: {token.scope}")
                    print(f"Expected: client scopes [{client.scope}]")
                    print(f"Actual: {token.scope}")
                except Token.DoesNotExist:
                    print("Token not found")


def test_client_credentials_without_scope():
    """Test client credentials grant without scope parameter.""" 
    print("\n=== Testing client_credentials grant WITHOUT scope ===")
    
    # Setup
    call_command('creatersakey')
    client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
    client.scope = ['openid', 'profile']  # Set some scopes
    client.save()
    
    c = Client()
    
    # Test without scope parameter
    post_data = {
        'grant_type': 'client_credentials',
        'client_id': client.client_id,
        'client_secret': client.client_secret,
    }
    
    response = c.post('/openid/token/', data=post_data)
    print(f"Response status: {response.status_code}")
    print(f"Response content: {response.content.decode('utf-8')}")
    
    if response.status_code == 200:
        response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
        access_token = response_dict.get('access_token')
        if access_token:
            # Check what scopes were actually assigned to the token
            from oidc_provider.models import Token
            try:
                token = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
                print(f"Token scopes: {token.scope}")
                print(f"Expected: client scopes [{client.scope}]")
                print(f"Actual: {token.scope}")
            except Token.DoesNotExist:
                print("Token not found")


def test_password_grant_with_invalid_scope():
    """Test password grant with invalid scope."""
    print("\n=== Testing password grant WITH invalid scope ===")
    
    # Setup
    call_command('creatersakey')
    user = create_fake_user()
    client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
    # Client has no specific scopes set
    
    with override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True):
        c = Client()
        
        post_data = {
            'username': 'johndoe',
            'password': '1234',
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'scope': 'invalid_scope',
            'client_id': client.client_id,
            'client_secret': client.client_secret,
        }
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', data=post_data)
        print(f"Response status: {response.status_code}")
        print(f"Response content: {response.content.decode('utf-8')}")


def test_introspection_scope_display():
    """Test if scope is displayed in introspection response."""
    print("\n=== Testing introspection scope display ===")
    
    # Setup
    call_command('creatersakey')
    user = create_fake_user()
    client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
    client.scope = ['openid', 'profile', 'token_introspection']
    client.save()
    
    # Create a resource client for introspection
    resource_client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
    resource_client.scope = ['token_introspection']
    resource_client.save()
    
    with override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True):
        c = Client()
        
        # Get a token first
        post_data = {
            'username': 'johndoe',
            'password': '1234',
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'scope': 'openid profile',
            'client_id': client.client_id,
            'client_secret': client.client_secret,
        }
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', data=post_data)
        print(f"Token response status: {response.status_code}")
        
        if response.status_code == 200:
            response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
            access_token = response_dict.get('access_token')
            
            if access_token:
                # Now introspect the token
                introspect_data = {
                    'token': access_token,
                    'client_id': resource_client.client_id,
                    'client_secret': resource_client.client_secret,
                }
                
                introspect_response = c.post('/openid/introspect/', data=introspect_data)
                print(f"Introspection response status: {introspect_response.status_code}")
                print(f"Introspection response: {introspect_response.content.decode('utf-8')}")
                
                # Check if scope is included
                introspect_dict = json.loads(introspect_response.content.decode('utf-8'))
                if 'scope' in introspect_dict:
                    print(f"Scope found in introspection: {introspect_dict['scope']}")
                else:
                    print("Scope NOT found in introspection response")


if __name__ == '__main__':

    test_client_credentials_without_scope() 
    test_password_grant_with_invalid_scope()

import django
import os
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '.')
os.environ.setdefault('DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE', 'oidc_provider.tests.settings')
django.setup()

from django.test import override_settings
from oidc_provider.tests.app.utils import create_fake_user, create_fake_client
from django.test import Client
import json

# Setup
from django.core.management import call_command
call_command('creatersakey')

user = create_fake_user()
client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')

with override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True):
    c = Client()
    
    # Test without scope parameter
    post_data = {
        'username': 'johndoe',
        'password': '1234',
        'grant_type': 'password',
        'client_id': client.client_id,
        'client_secret': client.client_secret,
    }
    
    response = c.post('/openid/token/', data=post_data)
    print('Response status:', response.status_code)
    if response.status_code == 200:
        response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
        access_token = response_dict.get('access_token')
        print('Access token:', access_token)
        
        # Check what scopes were actually assigned to the token
        from oidc_provider.models import Token
        try:
            token = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
            print('Token scopes:', token.scope)
            print('Client scopes:', client.scope)
            print('Expected behavior: Should be client scopes, but got:', token.scope)
        except Token.DoesNotExist:
            print('Token not found')
    else:
        print('Error response:', response.content.decode('utf-8'))

import django
import os
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '.')
os.environ.setdefault('DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE', 'oidc_provider.tests.settings')
django.setup()
from django.test import override_settings
from oidc_provider.tests.app.utils import create_fake_user, create_fake_client
from django.test import Client
import json
# Setup
from django.core.management import call_command
call_command('creatersakey')
user = create_fake_user()
client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
with override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True):
    c = Client()

    # Test without scope parameter
    post_data = {
        'username': 'johndoe',
        'password': '1234',
        'grant_type': 'password',
        'client_id': client.client_id,
        'client_secret': client.client_secret,
    }

    response = c.post('/openid/token/', data=post_data)
    print('Response status:', response.status_code)
    if response.status_code == 200:
        response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
        access_token = response_dict.get('access_token')
        print('Access token:', access_token)

        # Check what scopes were actually assigned to the token
        from oidc_provider.models import Token
        try:
            token = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
            print('Token scopes:', token.scope)
            print('Client scopes:', client.scope)
            print('Expected behavior: Should be client scopes, but got:', token.scope)

            print('Token not found')

The main issues are:
1. **Password Grant**: Assigns empty scope `['']` instead of client's scopes when no scope parameter provided
2. **Missing Scope Validation**: Neither password nor client_credentials grants validate requested scopes against client's allowed scopes
3. **Missing Introspection Scope**: Introspection responses don't include scope information

### 5.2 Problem Location
- **Token Endpoint**: `oidc_provider/lib/endpoints/token.py` lines 231-258 (password) and 260-275 (client_credentials)
- **Introspection Endpoint**: `oidc_provider/lib/endpoints/introspection.py` lines 81-94

### 5.3 How Test Reproduces Issue
The test `test_password_grant_get_access_token_without_scope` shows that password grant works without scope, but doesn't verify what scope is actually assigned to the token. The issue is that line 237 in token.py uses `self.params['scope'].split(' ')` which results in `['']` when scope is empty.

### 5.4 Best Practices

- Maintain backward compatibility
- Use existing `TokenError('invalid_scope')` error pattern
- Follow existing code style

4. **Settings**: Add new setting for introspection scope control

        # See https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc6749#section-4.3

        # Handle optional scope parameter
        scope_param = self.params['scope']
        if scope_param:
            scope = scope_param.split(' ')
            # Validate requested scopes against client's allowed scopes
            unauthorized_scopes = set(scope) - set(self.client.scope)
            if unauthorized_scopes:
                raise TokenError('invalid_scope')
        else:
            # If no scope provided, use client's default scopes
            scope = self.client.scope

        token = create_token(
            self.user,
            self.client,
            scope)

        id_token_dic = create_id_token(
            token=token,
            user=self.user,
            aud=self.client.client_id,
            nonce='self.code.nonce',
            at_hash=token.at_hash,
            request=self.request,
            scope=token.scope,
        )

        token.id_token = id_token_dic
        token.save()

        return {
            'access_token': token.access_token,
            'refresh_token': token.refresh_token,
            'expires_in': settings.get('OIDC_TOKEN_EXPIRE'),
            'token_type': 'bearer',
            'id_token': encode_id_token(id_token_dic, token.client),
        }</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/juanifioren__django-oidc-provider__0.7/oidc_provider/lib/endpoints/token.py has been edited. According to the OAuth2 spec, when no scopes are defined for a client, it should be able to request any scope. However, my implementation is being too restrictive. Let me check what the existing behavior was supposed to be by looking at how the refresh token handles this case.

Looking back at the refresh token logic, it validates against `self.token.scope`, which is the scope of the original token. But for password and client_credentials, we should validate against the client's allowed scopes.

The test clients don't have any specific scopes set, which likely means they should be able to request any scope.

Let me think about this differently:
1. If a client has specific scopes defined, then requested scopes should be validated against those
2. If a client has no scopes defined (empty _scope), then it should be able to request any scope
3. This makes sense from a practical standpoint - if you don't restrict a client's scopes, it should be able to request whatever it wants

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>So the default is an empty string. When `_scope` is empty, `scope` property returns `''` which splits to `[]` (empty list). This means if a client has no scopes defined, it has an empty scope list.

But looking at the logic, I think the right interpretation is:
1. If client has no scopes defined (empty list), it can request any scope
2. If client has specific scopes defined, requested scopes must be subset of those

        # See https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc6749#section-4.3

        # Handle optional scope parameter
        scope_param = self.params['scope']
        if scope_param:
            scope = scope_param.split(' ')
            # Validate requested scopes against client's allowed scopes
            # If client has scopes defined, requested scopes must be subset
            if self.client.scope and not set(scope).issubset(set(self.client.scope)):
                raise TokenError('invalid_scope')
        else:
            # If no scope provided, use client's default scopes
            scope = self.client.scope

            self.user,

            nonce='self.code.nonce',

            scope=token.scope,

        if scope_param:

            unauthorized_scopes = set(scope) - set(self.client.scope)
            if unauthorized_scopes:

"""
Comprehensive test to verify the scope handling fixes.
"""

import os
import sys
import django
from django.conf import settings

# Add the project root to Python path
sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace/juanifioren__django-oidc-provider__0.7')

# Configure Django settings
os.environ.setdefault('DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE', 'oidc_provider.tests.settings')
django.setup()

from django.test import TestCase, Client, override_settings
from django.core.management import call_command
import json

from oidc_provider.tests.app.utils import create_fake_user, create_fake_client
from oidc_provider.models import Token


def test_password_grant_without_scope():
    """Test password grant without scope - should use client scopes."""
    print("=== Testing password grant WITHOUT scope ===")
    
    # Setup
    call_command('creatersakey')
    user = create_fake_user()
    client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
    
    # Set some client scopes
    client.scope = ['openid', 'profile', 'email']
    client.save()
    
    with override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True):
        c = Client()
        
        # Test without scope parameter
        post_data = {
            'username': 'johndoe',
            'password': '1234',
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'client_id': client.client_id,
            'client_secret': client.client_secret,
        }
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', data=post_data)
        print(f"Response status: {response.status_code}")
        
        if response.status_code == 200:
            response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
            access_token = response_dict.get('access_token')
            if access_token:
                # Check what scopes were actually assigned to the token
                token = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
                print(f"Token scopes: {token.scope}")
                print(f"Expected: client scopes {client.scope}")
                assert token.scope == client.scope, f"Expected {client.scope}, got {token.scope}"
                print("✓ PASS: Correctly assigned client scopes when no scope provided")
                return True
            else:
                print("✗ FAIL: No access token in response")
                return False
        else:
            print(f"✗ FAIL: Expected 200, got {response.status_code}")
            print(f"Response: {response.content.decode('utf-8')}")
            return False


def test_password_grant_with_valid_scope():
    """Test password grant with valid scope."""
    print("\n=== Testing password grant WITH valid scope ===")
    
    # Setup
    call_command('creatersakey')
    user = create_fake_user()
    client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
    
    # Set some client scopes
    client.scope = ['openid', 'profile', 'email']
    client.save()
    
    with override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True):
        c = Client()
        
        # Test with valid scope parameter
        post_data = {
            'username': 'johndoe',
            'password': '1234',
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'scope': 'openid profile',
            'client_id': client.client_id,
            'client_secret': client.client_secret,
        }
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', data=post_data)
        print(f"Response status: {response.status_code}")
        
        if response.status_code == 200:
            response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
            access_token = response_dict.get('access_token')
            if access_token:
                # Check what scopes were actually assigned to the token
                token = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
                expected_scopes = ['openid', 'profile']
                print(f"Token scopes: {token.scope}")
                print(f"Expected: {expected_scopes}")
                assert token.scope == expected_scopes, f"Expected {expected_scopes}, got {token.scope}"
                print("✓ PASS: Correctly assigned requested scopes")
                return True
            else:
                print("✗ FAIL: No access token in response")
                return False
        else:
            print(f"✗ FAIL: Expected 200, got {response.status_code}")
            print(f"Response: {response.content.decode('utf-8')}")
            return False


def test_password_grant_with_invalid_scope():
    """Test password grant with invalid scope - should fail."""
    print("\n=== Testing password grant WITH invalid scope ===")
    
    # Setup
    call_command('creatersakey')
    user = create_fake_user()
    client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
    
    # Set some client scopes
    client.scope = ['openid', 'profile']
    client.save()
    
    with override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True):
        c = Client()
        
        # Test with invalid scope parameter
        post_data = {
            'username': 'johndoe',
            'password': '1234',
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'scope': 'invalid_scope',
            'client_id': client.client_id,
            'client_secret': client.client_secret,
        }
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', data=post_data)
        print(f"Response status: {response.status_code}")
        
        if response.status_code == 400:
            response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
            if response_dict.get('error') == 'invalid_scope':
                print("✓ PASS: Correctly rejected invalid scope")
                return True
            else:
                print(f"✗ FAIL: Expected invalid_scope error, got {response_dict}")
                return False
        else:
            print(f"✗ FAIL: Expected 400, got {response.status_code}")
            print(f"Response: {response.content.decode('utf-8')}")
            return False


def test_client_credentials_without_scope():
    """Test client_credentials grant without scope - should use client scopes."""
    print("\n=== Testing client_credentials grant WITHOUT scope ===")
    
    # Setup
    call_command('creatersakey')
    client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
    
    # Set some client scopes
    client.scope = ['read', 'write']
    client.save()
    
    c = Client()
    
    # Test without scope parameter
    post_data = {
        'grant_type': 'client_credentials',
        'client_id': client.client_id,
        'client_secret': client.client_secret,
    }
    
    response = c.post('/openid/token/', data=post_data)
    print(f"Response status: {response.status_code}")
    
    if response.status_code == 200:
        response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
        access_token = response_dict.get('access_token')
        scope_in_response = response_dict.get('scope')
        if access_token:
            # Check what scopes were actually assigned to the token
            token = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
            print(f"Token scopes: {token.scope}")
            print(f"Expected: client scopes {client.scope}")
            print(f"Scope in response: {scope_in_response}")
            expected_scope_string = ' '.join(client.scope)
            assert token.scope == client.scope, f"Expected {client.scope}, got {token.scope}"
            assert scope_in_response == expected_scope_string, f"Expected '{expected_scope_string}', got '{scope_in_response}'"
            print("✓ PASS: Correctly assigned client scopes when no scope provided")
            return True
        else:
            print("✗ FAIL: No access token in response")
            return False
    else:
        print(f"✗ FAIL: Expected 200, got {response.status_code}")
        print(f"Response: {response.content.decode('utf-8')}")
        return False


def test_client_credentials_with_valid_scope():
    """Test client_credentials grant with valid scope."""
    print("\n=== Testing client_credentials grant WITH valid scope ===")
    
    # Setup
    call_command('creatersakey')
    client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
    
    # Set some client scopes
    client.scope = ['read', 'write', 'delete']
    client.save()
    
    c = Client()
    
    # Test with valid scope parameter
    post_data = {
        'grant_type': 'client_credentials',
        'scope': 'read write',
        'client_id': client.client_id,
        'client_secret': client.client_secret,
    }
    
    response = c.post('/openid/token/', data=post_data)
    print(f"Response status: {response.status_code}")
    
    if response.status_code == 200:
        response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
        access_token = response_dict.get('access_token')
        scope_in_response = response_dict.get('scope')
        if access_token:
            # Check what scopes were actually assigned to the token
            token = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
            expected_scopes = ['read', 'write']
            expected_scope_string = ' '.join(expected_scopes)
            print(f"Token scopes: {token.scope}")
            print(f"Expected: {expected_scopes}")
            print(f"Scope in response: {scope_in_response}")
            assert token.scope == expected_scopes, f"Expected {expected_scopes}, got {token.scope}"
            assert scope_in_response == expected_scope_string, f"Expected '{expected_scope_string}', got '{scope_in_response}'"
            print("✓ PASS: Correctly assigned requested scopes")
            return True
        else:
            print("✗ FAIL: No access token in response")
            return False
    else:
        print(f"✗ FAIL: Expected 200, got {response.status_code}")
        print(f"Response: {response.content.decode('utf-8')}")
        return False

    response = c.post('/openid/token/', data=post_data)
    print(f"Response status: {response.status_code}")
    
    if response.status_code == 400:
        response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
        if response_dict.get('error') == 'invalid_scope':
            print("✓ PASS: Correctly rejected invalid scope")
            return True
        else:
            print(f"✗ FAIL: Expected invalid_scope error, got {response_dict}")
            return False
    else:
        print(f"✗ FAIL: Expected 400, got {response.status_code}")
        print(f"Response: {response.content.decode('utf-8')}")
        return False


def test_introspection_scope_display_disabled():
    """Test that scope is NOT displayed in introspection when setting is disabled (default)."""
    print("\n=== Testing introspection scope display DISABLED ===")
    
    # Setup
    call_command('creatersakey')
    user = create_fake_user()
    client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
    client.scope = ['openid', 'profile', 'token_introspection']
    client.save()
    
    # Create a resource client for introspection
    resource_client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
    resource_client.scope = ['token_introspection']
    resource_client.save()
    
    with override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True):
        c = Client()
        
        # Get a token first
        post_data = {
            'username': 'johndoe',
            'password': '1234',
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'scope': 'openid profile',
            'client_id': client.client_id,
            'client_secret': client.client_secret,
        }
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', data=post_data)
        print(f"Token response status: {response.status_code}")
        
        if response.status_code == 200:
            response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
            access_token = response_dict.get('access_token')
            
            if access_token:
                # Now introspect the token
                introspect_data = {
                    'token': access_token,
                    'client_id': resource_client.client_id,
                    'client_secret': resource_client.client_secret,
                }
                
                introspect_response = c.post('/openid/introspect/', data=introspect_data)
                print(f"Introspection response status: {introspect_response.status_code}")
                
                if introspect_response.status_code == 200:
                    introspect_dict = json.loads(introspect_response.content.decode('utf-8'))
                    if 'scope' not in introspect_dict:
                        print("✓ PASS: Scope NOT included in introspection response (setting disabled)")
                        return True
                    else:
                        print(f"✗ FAIL: Scope should not be included, but got: {introspect_dict.get('scope')}")
                        return False
                else:
                    print(f"✗ FAIL: Introspection failed with status {introspect_response.status_code}")
                    return False
            else:
                print("✗ FAIL: No access token received")
                return False
        else:
            print(f"✗ FAIL: Token request failed with status {response.status_code}")
            return False


def test_introspection_scope_display_enabled():
    """Test that scope IS displayed in introspection when setting is enabled."""
    print("\n=== Testing introspection scope display ENABLED ===")
    
    # Setup
    call_command('creatersakey')
    user = create_fake_user()
    client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
    client.scope = ['openid', 'profile', 'token_introspection']
    client.save()
    
    # Create a resource client for introspection
    resource_client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
    resource_client.scope = ['token_introspection']
    resource_client.save()
    
    with override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True, 
                          OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE=True):
        c = Client()
        
        # Get a token first
        post_data = {
            'username': 'johndoe',
            'password': '1234',
            'grant_type': 'password',
            'scope': 'openid profile',
            'client_id': client.client_id,
            'client_secret': client.client_secret,
        }
        
        response = c.post('/openid/token/', data=post_data)
        print(f"Token response status: {response.status_code}")
        
        if response.status_code == 200:
            response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
            access_token = response_dict.get('access_token')
            
            if access_token:
                # Now introspect the token
                introspect_data = {
                    'token': access_token,
                    'client_id': resource_client.client_id,
                    'client_secret': resource_client.client_secret,
                }
                
                introspect_response = c.post('/openid/introspect/', data=introspect_data)
                print(f"Introspection response status: {introspect_response.status_code}")
                
                if introspect_response.status_code == 200:
                    introspect_dict = json.loads(introspect_response.content.decode('utf-8'))
                    if 'scope' in introspect_dict:
                        expected_scope = 'openid profile'
                        actual_scope = introspect_dict.get('scope')
                        print(f"Scope in introspection: {actual_scope}")
                        if actual_scope == expected_scope:
                            print("✓ PASS: Scope correctly included in introspection response")
                            return True
                        else:
                            print(f"✗ FAIL: Expected scope '{expected_scope}', got '{actual_scope}'")
                            return False
                    else:
                        print("✗ FAIL: Scope should be included but is missing")
                        return False
                else:
                    print(f"✗ FAIL: Introspection failed with status {introspect_response.status_code}")
                    return False
            else:
                print("✗ FAIL: No access token received")
                return False
        else:
            print(f"✗ FAIL: Token request failed with status {response.status_code}")
            return False

"""
Test to verify the new scope behavior works as expected.
This mimics the existing test structure but focuses on our new functionality.
"""

import json
import uuid
from django.test import TestCase, override_settings
from django.core.management import call_command
try:
    from django.urls import reverse
except ImportError:
    from django.core.urlresolvers import reverse

from oidc_provider.tests.app.utils import (
    create_fake_user,
    create_fake_client,
)
from oidc_provider.models import Token


class NewScopeBehaviorTestCase(TestCase):
    """
    Tests for the enhanced scope handling functionality.
    """

    def setUp(self):
        call_command('creatersakey')
        self.user = create_fake_user()
        self.client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')

    def _password_grant_post_data(self, scope=None):
        result = {
            'username': 'johndoe',
            'password': '1234',
            'grant_type': 'password',
        }
        if scope is not None:
            result['scope'] = ' '.join(scope)
        return result

    def _password_grant_auth_header(self):
        return {
            'HTTP_AUTHORIZATION': 'Basic ' + (
                (self.client.client_id + ':' + self.client.client_secret)
                .encode('utf-8').decode('utf-8')
            ),
        }

    def _post_request(self, post_data, extras={}):
        url = reverse('oidc_provider:token')
        return self.client.post(url, data=post_data, **extras)

    def test_password_grant_assigns_client_scopes_when_no_scope_provided(self):
        """Test that password grant assigns client scopes when no scope parameter is provided."""
        # Set client scopes
        self.client.scope = ['openid', 'profile', 'email']
        self.client.save()
        
        with override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True):
            post_data = self._password_grant_post_data()
            response = self._post_request(
                post_data=post_data,
                extras=self._password_grant_auth_header()
            )

            self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 200)
            response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
            self.assertIn('access_token', response_dict)
            
            # Check that the token has the client's scopes
            access_token = response_dict['access_token']
            token = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
            self.assertEqual(token.scope, self.client.scope)

    def test_password_grant_with_valid_requested_scopes(self):
        """Test that password grant works with valid requested scopes."""
        # Set client scopes
        self.client.scope = ['openid', 'profile', 'email']
        self.client.save()
        
        with override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True):
            post_data = self._password_grant_post_data(scope=['openid', 'profile'])
            response = self._post_request(
                post_data=post_data,
                extras=self._password_grant_auth_header()
            )

            self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 200)
            response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
            self.assertIn('access_token', response_dict)
            
            # Check that the token has the requested scopes
            access_token = response_dict['access_token']
            token = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
            self.assertEqual(token.scope, ['openid', 'profile'])

    def test_password_grant_rejects_invalid_scopes(self):
        """Test that password grant rejects invalid scopes."""
        # Set client scopes
        self.client.scope = ['openid', 'profile']
        self.client.save()
        
        with override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True):
            post_data = self._password_grant_post_data(scope=['invalid_scope'])
            response = self._post_request(
                post_data=post_data,
                extras=self._password_grant_auth_header()
            )

            self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 400)
            response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
            self.assertEqual(response_dict['error'], 'invalid_scope')

    def test_client_credentials_assigns_client_scopes_when_no_scope_provided(self):
        """Test that client_credentials grant assigns client scopes when no scope parameter is provided."""
        # Set client scopes
        self.client.scope = ['read', 'write']
        self.client.save()
        
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'client_credentials',
            'client_id': self.client.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client.client_secret,
        }
        response = self._post_request(post_data)

        self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 200)
        response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
        self.assertIn('access_token', response_dict)
        self.assertIn('scope', response_dict)
        
        # Check that the token has the client's scopes
        access_token = response_dict['access_token']
        token = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
        self.assertEqual(token.scope, self.client.scope)
        self.assertEqual(response_dict['scope'], ' '.join(self.client.scope))

    def test_client_credentials_with_valid_requested_scopes(self):
        """Test that client_credentials grant works with valid requested scopes."""
        # Set client scopes
        self.client.scope = ['read', 'write', 'delete']
        self.client.save()
        
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'client_credentials',
            'scope': 'read write',
            'client_id': self.client.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client.client_secret,
        }
        response = self._post_request(post_data)

        self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 200)
        response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
        self.assertIn('access_token', response_dict)
        self.assertIn('scope', response_dict)
        
        # Check that the token has the requested scopes
        access_token = response_dict['access_token']
        token = Token.objects.get(access_token=access_token)
        self.assertEqual(token.scope, ['read', 'write'])
        self.assertEqual(response_dict['scope'], 'read write')

    def test_client_credentials_rejects_invalid_scopes(self):
        """Test that client_credentials grant rejects invalid scopes."""
        # Set client scopes
        self.client.scope = ['read', 'write']
        self.client.save()
        
        post_data = {
            'grant_type': 'client_credentials',
            'scope': 'invalid_scope',
            'client_id': self.client.client_id,
            'client_secret': self.client.client_secret,
        }
        response = self._post_request(post_data)

        self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 400)
        response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
        self.assertEqual(response_dict['error'], 'invalid_scope')

    @override_settings(OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE=True)
    def test_introspection_includes_scope_when_enabled(self):
        """Test that introspection includes scope when OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE is True."""
        # Set up clients
        self.client.scope = ['openid', 'profile', 'token_introspection']
        self.client.save()
        
        # Create resource client for introspection
        resource_client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
        resource_client.scope = ['token_introspection']
        resource_client.save()
        
        with override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True):
            # Get a token first
            post_data = {
                'username': 'johndoe',
                'password': '1234',
                'grant_type': 'password',
                'scope': 'openid profile',
                'client_id': self.client.client_id,
                'client_secret': self.client.client_secret,
            }
            response = self._post_request(
                post_data=post_data,
                extras=self._password_grant_auth_header()
            )
            
            self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 200)
            response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
            access_token = response_dict['access_token']
            
            # Now introspect the token
            introspect_data = {
                'token': access_token,
                'client_id': resource_client.client_id,
                'client_secret': resource_client.client_secret,
            }
            introspect_url = reverse('oidc_provider:token-introspection')
            introspect_response = self.client.post(introspect_url, data=introspect_data)
            
            self.assertEqual(introspect_response.status_code, 200)
            introspect_dict = json.loads(introspect_response.content.decode('utf-8'))
            self.assertTrue(introspect_dict['active'])
            self.assertIn('scope', introspect_dict)
            self.assertEqual(introspect_dict['scope'], 'openid profile')

    @override_settings(OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE=False)
    def test_introspection_excludes_scope_when_disabled(self):
        """Test that introspection excludes scope when OIDC_INTROSPECTION_RESPONSE_SCOPE_ENABLE is False."""
        # Set up clients
        self.client.scope = ['openid', 'profile', 'token_introspection']
        self.client.save()
        
        # Create resource client for introspection
        resource_client = create_fake_client(response_type='code')
        resource_client.scope = ['token_introspection']
        resource_client.save()
        
        with override_settings(OIDC_GRANT_TYPE_PASSWORD_ENABLE=True):
            # Get a token first
            post_data = {
                'username': 'johndoe',
                'password': '1234',
                'grant_type': 'password',
                'scope': 'openid profile',
                'client_id': self.client.client_id,
                'client_secret': self.client.client_secret,
            }
            response = self._post_request(
                post_data=post_data,
                extras=self._password_grant_auth_header()
            )
            
            self.assertEqual(response.status_code, 200)
            response_dict = json.loads(response.content.decode('utf-8'))
            access_token = response_dict['access_token']
            
            # Now introspect the token
            introspect_data = {
                'token': access_token,
                'client_id': resource_client.client_id,
                'client_secret': resource_client.client_secret,
            }
            introspect_url = reverse('oidc_provider:token-introspection')
            introspect_response = self.client.post(introspect_url, data=introspect_data)
            
            self.assertEqual(introspect_response.status_code, 200)
            introspect_dict = json.loads(introspect_response.content.decode('utf-8'))
            self.assertTrue(introspect_dict['active'])
            self.assertNotIn('scope', introspect_dict)</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/juanifioren__django-oidc-provider__0.7/test_new_scope_behavior.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think>
